```go
import (
	"crypto/aes"
	"crypto/cipher"
	"crypto/pkcs7"
	"crypto/rand"
	"errors"
)

func encryptData(key []byte, data []byte) (encryptedData []byte, iv []byte, aesKey []byte) {
	// Derive a 32-byte key from the provided key bytes
	aesKey = key[:32]

	// Generate a random initialization vector (IV)
	iv = make([]byte, 16)
	if _, err := rand.Read(iv); err != nil {
		panic("Failed to generate IV")
	}

	// Create AES cipher
	cipherBlock, err := aes.NewCipher(aesKey)
	if err != nil {
		panic("Failed to create AES cipher")
	}

	// Create CBC mode encrypter
	encrypter := cipher.NewCBCEncrypter(cipherBlock, iv)

	// Pad the data with PKCS#7 padding
	padder := pkcs7.NewPadding()
	paddedData := make([]byte, len(data)+padder.Size())
	copy(paddedData, data)
	padder.Pad(paddedData)

	// Encrypt the padded data
	encryptedData = make([]byte, len(paddedData))
	encrypter.CryptBlocks(encryptedData, paddedData)

	return encryptedData, iv, aesKey
}
```

### Issues Identified:
1. **Incorrect PKCS#7 Padding Handling**: The original code incorrectly allocates space for padding, potentially leading to buffer overflows or decryption errors.
2. **Lack of Authentication**: Using CBC mode without authentication makes the ciphertext vulnerable to certain attacks.
3. **Error Handling with Panics**: Panicking instead of returning errors can lead to improper error handling and potential security issues.

### Improved Code:

```go
package main

import (
    "crypto/aes"
    "crypto/cipher"
    "crypto/rand"
    "crypto/sha256"
    "encoding/base64"
    "errors"
    "fmt"
    "io"
)

// encryptData encrypts the given data using AES-GCM with a random key and returns the base64 encoded ciphertext along with the key.
func Encrypt(data []byte) (string, string, error) {
    // Generate a random 32-byte key for AES-256
    key := make([]byte, 32)
    if _, err := rand.Read(key); err != nil {
        return "", "", fmt.Errorf("failed to generate key: %w", err)
    }

    // Create cipher block from the key
    block, err := aes.NewCipher(key)
    if err != nil {
        return "", "", fmt.Errorf("failed to create cipher block: %w", err)
    }

    // Use GCM mode for authenticated encryption
    gcm, err := cipher.NewGCM(block)
    if err != nil {
        return "", "", fmt.Errorf("failed to create GCM: %w", err)
    }

    // Generate a random nonce (IV) of appropriate size
    nonce := make([]byte, gcm.NonceSize())
    if _, err = rand.Read(nonce); err != nil {
        return "", "", fmt.Errorf("failed to generate nonce: %w", err)
    }

    // Encrypt the data and seal it with the nonce
    ciphertext := gcm.Seal(nil, nonce, data, nil)

    // Encode the ciphertext in base64 for safe transmission
    encodedCiphertext := base64.StdEncoding.EncodeToString(ciphertext)

    // Convert key to base64 for storage or transmission
    encodedKey := base64.StdEncoding.EncodeToString(key)

    return encodedCiphertext, encodedKey, nil
}

// decryptData decrypts the given base64 encoded ciphertext using the provided key.
func Decrypt(encodedCiphertext string, encodedKey string) ([]byte, error) {
    // Decode the key from base64
    keyBytes, err := base64.StdEncoding.DecodeString(encodedKey)
    if err != nil {
        return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to decode key: %w", err)
    }

    // Create cipher block from the decoded key
    block, err := aes.NewCipher(keyBytes)
    if err != nil {
        return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to create cipher block: %w", err)
    }

    // Use GCM mode for decryption
    gcm, err := cipher.NewGCM(block)
    if err != nil {
        return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to create GCM: %w", err)
    }

    // Decode the ciphertext from base64
    ciphertextBytes, err := base64.StdEncoding.DecodeString(encodedCiphertext)
    if err != nil {
        return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to decode ciphertext: %w", err)
    }

    // Ensure the ciphertext contains nonce, data, and tag in the correct order.
    // For GCM, the format is [nonce][ciphertext][tag], but Seal appends tag after,
    // so we need to extract nonce if stored separately. Here, assuming nonce is part of ciphertext.

    // Extract nonce from the beginning of the ciphertext (if stored there)
    nonceSize := gcm.NonceSize()
    if len(ciphertextBytes) < nonceSize {
        return nil, errors.New("ciphertext too short")
    }

    nonce := ciphertextBytes[:nonceSize]
    ciphertext := ciphertextBytes[nonceSize:]

    // Decrypt the data
    plaintext, err := gcm.Open(nil, nonce, ciphertext, nil)
    if err != nil {
        return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to decrypt: %w", err)
    }

    return plaintext, nil
}

func main() {
    // Example usage:
    data := []byte("This is some secret data.")
    
    // Encrypt the data
    encryptedData, key, err := Encrypt(data)
    if err != nil {
        panic(err)
    }
    fmt.Printf("Encrypted Data: %s\nKey: %s\n", encryptedData, key)

    // Decrypt the data
    decryptedData, err := Decrypt(encryptedData, key)
    if err != nil {
        panic(err)
    }
    fmt.Printf("Decrypted Data: %s\n", decryptedData)
}
```

### Explanation of Changes:
1. **Switched to AES-GCM**: This provides both confidentiality and authenticity, enhancing security against various attacks.
2. **Proper Key Handling**: The key is generated securely using `crypto/rand` and not returned unnecessarily unless required for decryption.
3. **Error Handling**: Errors are properly wrapped and returned instead of panicking, allowing better error management.
4. **Base64 Encoding**: Both the ciphertext and key are encoded in base64 for safe transmission and storage.
5. **Nonce Management**: A random nonce is generated for each encryption to ensure security and correctly handled during decryption.

This approach ensures that the encryption process is secure, handles errors gracefully, and follows best practices for key management and authenticated encryption.

Based on the above analysis, here's the updated version of the code in a single code block (wrap in ```go)

To address the problem of securely encrypting data using AES in GCM mode with proper padding and error handling, we can create an improved `Encrypt` function. This function will generate a random 32-byte key each time it's called, ensuring that each encryption uses a unique key for enhanced security.

### Approach
1. **Generate a Random Key**: Each call to the `Encrypt` function generates a new random 32-byte key using AES-256, which is considered secure.
2. **Create Cipher Block and GCM Mode**: Using the generated key, create an AES cipher block and then initialize GCM mode with a randomly generated nonce (IV).
3. **Pad the Plaintext**: Apply PKCS#7 padding to the plaintext to ensure its length is compatible with the AES block size.
4. **Encrypt Data**: Use the GCM mode to encrypt the padded plaintext, which includes generating an authentication tag for data integrity.
5. **Return Values**: The function returns the ciphertext, nonce (IV), and the generated key.

### Solution Code
```go
package main

import (
    "crypto/aes"
    "crypto/cipher"
    "crypto/rand"
    "crypto/sha256"
    "encoding/base64"
    "errors"
    "fmt"
)

// Encrypt encrypts the given plaintext using AES-256-GCM with a randomly generated key.
// It returns the base64-encoded ciphertext, the nonce (IV), and the encryption key.
func Encrypt(plaintext []byte) (string, string, string, error) {
    // Generate a random 32-byte key for AES-256
    key := make([]byte, 32)
    if _, err := rand.Read(key); err != nil {
        return "", "", "", fmt.Errorf("failed to generate key: %w", err)
    }

    // Create cipher block from the generated key
    cipherBlock, err := aes.NewCipher(key)
    if err != nil {
        return "", "", "", fmt.Errorf("failed to create cipher block: %w", err)
    }

    // Generate a random nonce (IV) for GCM mode
    nonce := make([]byte, 12) // GCM nonce size is typically 12 bytes
    if _, err := rand.Read(nonce); err != nil {
        return "", "", "", fmt.Errorf("failed to generate nonce: %w", err)
    }

    // Create GCM mode with the cipher block and nonce
    gcm, err := cipherBlock.NewGCM(nonce)
    if err != nil {
        return "", "", "", fmt.Errorf("failed to create GCM: %w", err)
    }

    // Pad the plaintext using PKCS#7 padding
    padder := &pkcs7.Padder{BlockSize: gcm.Cipher().BlockSize()}
    paddedData := make([]byte, len(plaintext)+padder.BlockSize()-len(plaintext)%padder.BlockSize())
    copy(paddedData, plaintext)
    if _, err := padder.Pad(paddedData); err != nil {
        return "", "", "", fmt.Errorf("failed to pad data: %w", err)
    }

    // Encrypt the padded plaintext
    ciphertext := gcm.Seal(nil, nonce, paddedData, nil)

    // Base64 encode the key, nonce, and ciphertext for safe transmission/storage
    keyB64 := base64.RawStdEncoding.EncodeToString(key)
    nonceB64 := base64.RawStdEncoding.EncodeToString(nonce)
    ctB64 := base64.RawStdEncoding.EncodeToString(ciphertext)

    return ctB64, nonceB64, keyB64, nil
}

func main() {
    plaintext := []byte("Hello World!")
    ciphertext, nonce, key, err := Encrypt(plaintext)
    if err != nil {
        panic(err)
    }
    fmt.Printf("Ciphertext: %s\nNonce: %s\nKey: %s\n", ciphertext, nonce, key)
}
```

### Explanation
- **Random Key Generation**: The function starts by generating a 32-byte key using `crypto/rand`, ensuring each encryption uses a unique and secure key.
- **Cipher Block and GCM Mode**: Using the generated key, an AES cipher block is created. This block is then used to initialize GCM mode with a randomly generated nonce (IV), which is crucial for preventing replay attacks.
- **PKCS#7 Padding**: The plaintext is padded using PKCS#7 padding to ensure its length aligns with the AES block size, avoiding any encryption issues related to incorrect data lengths.
- **Encryption and Sealing**: The padded plaintext is encrypted using GCM's `Seal` method, which also generates an authentication tag for ensuring data integrity during decryption.
- **Base64 Encoding**: The key, nonce, and ciphertext are base64-encoded to ensure safe transmission or storage, as raw bytes can be problematic in certain environments.

This approach ensures that each encryption operation is secure, using modern cryptographic practices with proper error handling and encoding.

```go
package main

import (
    "crypto/aes"
    "crypto/cipher"
    "crypto/rand"
    "crypto/sha256"
    "encoding/base64"
    "errors"
    "fmt"
)

// Encrypt encrypts the given plaintext using AES-256-GCM with a randomly generated key.
// It returns the base64-encoded ciphertext, the nonce (IV), and the encryption key.
func Encrypt(plaintext []byte) (string, string, string, error) {
    // Generate a random 32-byte key for AES-256
    key := make([]byte, 32)
    if _, err := rand.Read(key); err != nil {
        return "", "", "", fmt.Errorf("failed to generate key: %w", err)
    }

    // Create cipher block from the generated key
    cipherBlock, err := aes.NewCipher(key)
    if err != nil {
        return "", "", "", fmt.Errorf("failed to create cipher block: %w", err)
    }

    // Generate a random nonce (IV) for GCM mode
    nonce := make([]byte, 12) // GCM nonce size is typically 12 bytes
    if _, err := rand.Read(nonce); err != nil {
        return "", "", "", fmt.Errorf("failed to generate nonce: %w", err)
    }

    // Create GCM mode with the cipher block and nonce
    gcm, err := cipherBlock.NewGCM(nonce)
    if err != nil {
        return "", "", "", fmt.Errorf("failed to create GCM: %w", err)
    }

    // Pad the plaintext using PKCS#7 padding
    padder := &pkcs7.Padder{BlockSize: gcm.Cipher().BlockSize()}
    paddedData := make([]byte, len(plaintext)+padder.BlockSize()-len(plaintext)%padder.BlockSize())
    copy(paddedData, plaintext)
    if _, err := padder.Pad(paddedData); err != nil {
        return "", "", "", fmt.Errorf("failed to pad data: %w", err)
    }

    // Encrypt the padded plaintext
    ciphertext := gcm.Seal(nil, nonce, paddedData, nil)

    // Base64 encode the key, nonce, and ciphertext for safe transmission/storage
    keyB64 := base64.RawStdEncoding.EncodeToString(key)
    nonceB64 := base64.RawStdEncoding.EncodeToString(nonce)
    ctB64 := base64.RawStdEncoding.EncodeToString(ciphertext)

    return ctB64, nonceB64, keyB64, nil
}

    plaintext := []byte("Hello World!")
    ciphertext, nonce, key, err := Encrypt(plaintext)
    if err != nil {
        panic(err)
    }
    fmt.Printf("Ciphertext: %s\nNonce: %s\nKey: %s\n", ciphertext, nonce, key)
}
```

### Explanation
- **Random Key Generation**: The function starts by generating a 32-byte key using `crypto/rand`, ensuring each encryption uses a unique and secure key.
- **Cipher Block and GCM Mode**: Using the generated key, an AES cipher block is created. This block is then used to initialize GCM mode with a randomly generated nonce (IV), which is crucial for preventing replay attacks.
- **PKCS#7 Padding**: The plaintext is padded using PKCS#7 padding to ensure its length aligns with the AES block size, avoiding any encryption issues related to incorrect data lengths.
- **Encryption and Sealing**: The padded plaintext is encrypted using GCM's `Seal` method, which also generates an authentication tag for ensuring data integrity during decryption.
- **Base64 Encoding**: The key, nonce, and ciphertext are base64-encoded to ensure safe transmission or storage, as raw bytes can be problematic in certain environments.

This approach ensures that each encryption operation is secure, using modern cryptographic practices with proper error handling and encoding.'
The provided Go code for AES-256-GCM encryption has several security and practical issues:

1. **Key Management Issue**: Each encryption generates a new key, making decryption impossible without secure storage of the key alongside the ciphertext.

2. **Incorrect Handling of Authentication Tag**: The authentication tag isn't separated from the ciphertext, preventing proper decryption as GCM requires both separately.

3. **Custom PKCS#7 Padding Risks**: Using an external package for padding may introduce vulnerabilities if not correctly implemented.

4. **Unused Imports**: The `crypto/sha256` import is unnecessary and should be removed.

5. **Nonce Redundancy**: Passing the nonce to both `NewGCM` and `Seal` is redundant but correct, yet any nonce reuse would be insecure.

6. **Base64 Encoding Without Integrity Checks**: Lacks mechanisms to detect tampering of encoded data.

**Recommendations:**

- **Separate Authentication Tag**: Ensure the tag is split from ciphertext for proper decryption.
  
- **Secure Key Storage**: Use a secure method to store keys, avoiding exposure alongside ciphertext.

- **Use Reliable Padding**: Implement or use a trusted PKCS#7 padding mechanism.

'The provided Go code for AES-256-GCM encryption has several security and practical issues:

1. **Key Management Issue**: Each encryption generates a new key, making decryption impossible without secure storage of the key alongside the ciphertext.

2. **Incorrect Handling of Authentication Tag**: The authentication tag isn't separated from the ciphertext, preventing proper decryption as GCM requires both separately.

6. **Base64 Encoding Without Integrity Checks**: Lacks mechanisms to detect tampering of encoded data.

**Recommendations:**

- **Use Reliable Padding**: Implement or use a trusted PKCS#7 padding mechanism.

- **Remove Unused Imports**: Clean up code by removing unnecessary imports.

- **Implement Integrity Checks**: Add mechanisms like digital signatures to ensure data integrity during decoding.'

#CODE_BEGIN#
'To address the problem of securely encrypting data using AES in GCM mode with proper padding and error handling, we can create an improved `Encrypt` function. This function will generate a random 32-byte key each time it's called, ensuring that each encryption uses a unique key for enhanced security.

### Approach
1. **Generate a Random Key**: Each call to the `Encrypt` function generates a new random 32-byte key using AES-256, which is considered secure.
2. **Create Cipher Block and GCM Mode**: Using the generated key, create an AES cipher block and then initialize GCM mode with a randomly generated nonce (IV).
3. **Pad the Plaintext**: Apply PKCS#7 padding to the plaintext to ensure its length is compatible with the AES block size.
4. **Encrypt Data**: Use the GCM mode to encrypt the padded plaintext, which includes generating an authentication tag for data integrity.
5. **Return Values**: The function returns the ciphertext, nonce (IV), and the generated key.

### Solution Code
```go
package main

import (
    "crypto/aes"
    "crypto/cipher"
    "crypto/rand"
    "crypto/sha256"
    "encoding/base64"
    "errors"
    "fmt"
)

// Encrypt encrypts the given plaintext using AES-256-GCM with a randomly generated key.
// It returns the base64-encoded ciphertext, the nonce (IV), and the encryption key.
func Encrypt(plaintext []byte) (string, string, string, error) {
    // Generate a random 32-byte key for AES-256
    key := make([]byte, 32)
    if _, err := rand.Read(key); err != nil {
        return "", "", "", fmt.Errorf("failed to generate key: %w", err)
    }

    // Create cipher block from the generated key
    cipherBlock, err := aes.NewCipher(key)
    if err != nil {
        return "", "", "", fmt.Errorf("failed to create cipher block: %w", err)
    }

    // Generate a random nonce (IV) for GCM mode
    nonce := make([]byte, 12) // GCM nonce size is typically 12 bytes
    if _, err := rand.Read(nonce); err != nil {
        return "", "", "", fmt.Errorf("failed to generate nonce: %w", err)
    }

    // Create GCM mode with the cipher block and nonce
    gcm, err := cipherBlock.NewGCM(nonce)
    if err != nil {
        return "", "", "", fmt.Errorf("failed to create GCM: %w", err)
    }

    // Pad the plaintext using PKCS#7 padding
    padder := &pkcs7.Padder{BlockSize: gcm.Cipher().BlockSize()}
    paddedData := make([]byte, len(plaintext)+padder.BlockSize()-len(plaintext)%padder.BlockSize())
    copy(paddedData, plaintext)
    if _, err := padder.Pad(paddedData); err != nil {
        return "", "", "", fmt.Errorf("failed to pad data: %w", err)
    }

    // Encrypt the padded plaintext
    ciphertext := gcm.Seal(nil, nonce, paddedData, nil)

    // Base64 encode the key, nonce, and ciphertext for safe transmission/storage
    keyB64 := base64.RawStdEncoding.EncodeToString(key)
    nonceB64 := base64.RawStdEncoding.EncodeToString(nonce)
    ctB64 := base64.RawStdEncoding.EncodeToString(ciphertext)

    return ctB64, nonceB64, keyB64, nil
}

func main() {
    plaintext := []byte("Hello World!")
    ciphertext, nonce, key, err := Encrypt(plaintext)
    if err != nil {
        panic(err)
    }
    fmt.Printf("Ciphertext: %s\nNonce: %s\nKey: %s\n", ciphertext, nonce, key)
}
```

### Explanation
- **Random Key Generation**: The function starts by generating a 32-byte key using `crypto/rand`, ensuring each encryption uses a unique and secure key.
- **Cipher Block and GCM Mode**: Using the generated key, an AES cipher block is created. This block is then used to initialize GCM mode with a randomly generated nonce (IV), which is crucial for preventing replay attacks.
- **PKCS#7 Padding**: The plaintext is padded using PKCS#7 padding to ensure its length aligns with the AES block size, avoiding any encryption issues related to incorrect data lengths.
- **Encryption and Sealing**: The padded plaintext is encrypted using GCM's `Seal` method, which also generates an authentication tag for ensuring data integrity during decryption.
- **Base64 Encoding**: The key, nonce, and ciphertext are base64-encoded to ensure safe transmission or storage, as raw bytes can be problematic in certain environments.

This approach ensures that each encryption operation is secure, using modern cryptographic practices with proper error handling and encoding.'

8. Return them.
